- **A.** $50^\circ$
- **B.** $25^\circ$
- **C.** $100^\circ$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $100^\circ$

1. Angle at the centre = 2 × angle at the circumference. $ \angle AOB = 2\times 50^\circ = 100^\circ $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $50^\circ$ — Treated as same-segment angles.
- **B** $25^\circ$ — Halved instead of doubling.

</details>

#### QZ-G10C31-F-ANGLE_CENT-04
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G10C31-F-ANGLE_CENT-04", "source_question": "MQ-G10C31-F-ANGLE_CENT-04", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G10C31", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-CIR-CENTRE", "diagram": "GEO-CIRC-CENTRE-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** O is the centre of the circle. The angle subtended by arc AB at a point C on the circumference is 35°. Find ∠AOB.

- **A.** $35^\circ$
- **B.** $70^\circ$
- **C.** $17^\circ$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $70^\circ$

1. Angle at the centre = 2 × angle at the circumference. $ \angle AOB = 2\times 35^\circ = 70^\circ $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $35^\circ$ — Treated as same-segment angles.
- **C** $17^\circ$ — Halved instead of doubling.

</details>

#### QZ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-01", "source_question": "MQ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-01", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G10C31", "value": 29, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "°", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-CIR-SEMI", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** AB is a diameter of a circle and C is a point on the circle. ∠CAB = 61°. Find ∠ABC.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$29^\circ$ (Unit: °)

1. Angle in a semicircle is 90°. $ \angle ACB = 90^\circ $
2. Angle sum of ΔABC. $ \angle ABC = 180 - 90 - 61 = 29^\circ $

</details>

#### QZ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-02", "source_question": "MQ-G10C31-G-ANGLE_SEMI-02", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G10C31", "value": 42, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "°", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-CIR-SEMI", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** AB is a diameter of a circle and C is a point on the circle. ∠CAB = 48°. Find ∠ABC.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$42^\circ$ (Unit: °)

1. Angle in a semicircle is 90°. $ \angle ACB = 90^\circ $
2. Angle sum of ΔABC. $ \angle ABC = 180 - 90 - 48 = 42^\circ $

</details>


### G10C32 — Scale Diagrams

#### QZ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-01", "source_question": "MQ-G10C32-F-SCALE_CONV-01", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G10C32", "value": 7.0, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "m", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-TRI-SCALE", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** On a scale diagram drawn to 1 : 100, a length is 7 cm. Find the actual length in metres.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$7\ \text{m}$ (Unit: m)

2. Convert cm to m (÷ 100). $ 700\div 100 = 7\ \text{m} $

</details>

#### QZ-G10C32-G-BEARING_BA-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G10C32-G-BEARING_BA-02", "source_question": "MQ-G10C32-G-BEARING_BA-02", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G10C32", "value": 300, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "°", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-TRI-BEAR", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** The bearing of B from A is 120°. Find the bearing of A from B.

### G11C01 — Real Numbers

#### QZ-G11C01-G-SIMPLIFY_S-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C01-G-SIMPLIFY_S-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C01-G-SIMPLIFY_S-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C01", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-NUM-SURD", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Write $\sqrt{72}$ in its simplest surd form.

- **A.** $36\times 2$
- **B.** $\sqrt{36}\times\sqrt{2}$
- **C.** $2\sqrt{6}$
- **D.** $6\sqrt{2}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**D** — $6\sqrt{2}$

1. Find the largest square factor. $ 72 = 36\times 2 $
2. Split the root. $ \sqrt{72} = \sqrt{36}\times\sqrt{2} $
3. Simplify. $ = 6\sqrt{2} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $36\times 2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $\sqrt{36}\times\sqrt{2}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $2\sqrt{6}$ — Square factor and other factor swapped.

</details>

1. Find the largest square factor. $ 45 = 9\times 5 $
2. Split the root. $ \sqrt{45} = \sqrt{9}\times\sqrt{5} $
3. Simplify. $ = 3\sqrt{5} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\sqrt{9}\times\sqrt{5}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $9\times 5$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $5\sqrt{3}$ — Square factor and other factor swapped.

</details>

### G11C02 — Indices and Logarithms I

#### QZ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C02", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-IDX-LAWS", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Find the value of $16^{-\frac{3}{4}}$.

- **A.** $\frac{1}{8}$
- **B.** $\frac{1}{16^{\frac{3}{4}}}$
- **C.** $8$
- **D.** $2$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $\frac{1}{8}$

1. Negative index → reciprocal. $ 16^{-\frac{3}{4}} = \frac{1}{16^{\frac{3}{4}}} $
2. Denominator 4 → 4th root. $ \sqrt[4]{16} = 2 $
3. Numerator 3 → power. $ 2^{3} = 8 $
4. Answer. $ \frac{1}{8} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $\frac{1}{16^{\frac{3}{4}}}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $8$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C02", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-IDX-LAWS", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Find the value of $27^{-\frac{2}{3}}$.

- **A.** $\frac{1}{27^{\frac{2}{3}}}$
- **B.** $9$
- **C.** $\frac{1}{9}$
- **D.** $3$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $\frac{1}{9}$

1. Negative index → reciprocal. $ 27^{-\frac{2}{3}} = \frac{1}{27^{\frac{2}{3}}} $
2. Denominator 3 → 3th root. $ \sqrt[3]{27} = 3 $
3. Numerator 2 → power. $ 3^{2} = 9 $
4. Answer. $ \frac{1}{9} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\frac{1}{27^{\frac{2}{3}}}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $9$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $3$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C02", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-IDX-LAWS", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Find the value of $8^{-\frac{2}{3}}$.

- **A.** $\frac{1}{8^{\frac{2}{3}}}$
- **B.** $\frac{1}{4}$
- **C.** $4$
- **D.** $2$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $\frac{1}{4}$

1. Negative index → reciprocal. $ 8^{-\frac{2}{3}} = \frac{1}{8^{\frac{2}{3}}} $
2. Denominator 3 → 3th root. $ \sqrt[3]{8} = 2 $
3. Numerator 2 → power. $ 2^{2} = 4 $
4. Answer. $ \frac{1}{4} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\frac{1}{8^{\frac{2}{3}}}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $4$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-04", "source_question": "MQ-G11C02-G-FRAC_NEG_I-04", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C02", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-IDX-LAWS", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->

- **C.** $8$
- **D.** $2$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $\frac{1}{8}$

1. Negative index → reciprocal. $ 32^{-\frac{3}{5}} = \frac{1}{32^{\frac{3}{5}}} $
2. Denominator 5 → 5th root. $ \sqrt[5]{32} = 2 $
3. Numerator 3 → power. $ 2^{3} = 8 $
4. Answer. $ \frac{1}{8} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $\frac{1}{32^{\frac{3}{5}}}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $8$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

#### QZ-G11C02-A-LOG_EXPR-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C02-A-LOG_EXPR-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C02-A-LOG_EXPR-01", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C02", "value": 2, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-LOG-LAWS", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Simplify: $\lg 8 + 2\lg 5 - \lg 2$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$2$ ()

1. Power rule: 2 lg 5 = lg 5² = lg 25. $ \lg 8 + \lg 25 - \lg 2 $
2. Combine: add → multiply, subtract → divide. $ \lg\frac{8\times 25}{2} = \lg 100 $
3. lg 100 = 2. $ = 2 $

</details>


### G11C03 — Indices and Logarithms II

#### QZ-G11C03-F-LOG_POWER-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C03-F-LOG_POWER-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C03-F-LOG_POWER-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C03", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-LOG-TAB", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->

**Why the other options are wrong:**

#### QZ-G11C03-G-LOG_ROOT-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C03-G-LOG_ROOT-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C03-G-LOG_ROOT-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C03", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-LOG-TAB", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Using logarithms, find $\sqrt[3]{0.343}$.

- **A.** $\frac{1}{3}\lg 0.343$
- **B.** $\bar{2} + 1.5353$
- **C.** $0.700$
- **D.** $\bar{1}.5353$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $0.700$

1. lg of a root = lg ÷ index. $ \lg P = \frac{1}{3}\lg 0.343 $
2. Write lg with a bar characteristic. $ \lg 0.343 = \bar{1}.5353 $
3. Make the characteristic divisible by 3. $ = \bar{2} + 1.5353 $
4. Divide by 3. $ \lg P = \bar{1}.5118 $
5. Antilog. $ P \approx 0.700 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\frac{1}{3}\lg 0.343$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $\bar{2} + 1.5353$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $\bar{1}.5353$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

2. Write lg with a bar characteristic. $ \lg 0.0343 = \bar{2}.5353 $
3. Make the characteristic divisible by 3. $ = \bar{4} + 2.5353 $
4. Divide by 3. $ \lg P = \bar{2}.8451 $
5. Antilog. $ P \approx 0.325 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\frac{1}{3}\lg 0.0343$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $\bar{4} + 2.5353$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

#### QZ-G11C04-G-CONE_CSA-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C04-G-CONE_CSA-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C04-G-CONE_CSA-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C04", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-MEN-CONEA", "diagram": "SOL-CONE-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** A right circular cone has base radius 7 cm and height 24 cm. Find the slant height and the curved surface area. (π = 22/7)

- **A.** $25$
- **B.** $l=25\text{ cm},\ 550\ \text{cm}^2$
- **C.** $528\ \text{cm}^2$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $l=25\text{ cm},\ 550\ \text{cm}^2$

1. Slant height by Pythagoras. $ l = \sqrt{7^2 + 24^2} = \sqrt{625} = 25 $
2. Curved surface area = πrl. $ \frac{22}{7}\times 7\times 25 = 550\ \text{cm}^2 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $25$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $528\ \text{cm}^2$ — Used vertical height h instead of l.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C04-G-CONE_CSA-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C04-G-CONE_CSA-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C04-G-CONE_CSA-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C04", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-MEN-CONEA", "diagram": "SOL-CONE-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** A right circular cone has base radius 9 cm and height 12 cm. Find the slant height and the curved surface area. (π = 22/7)

- **A.** $l=15\text{ cm},\ \frac{2970}{7}\ \text{cm}^2$
- **B.** $\frac{2376}{7}\ \text{cm}^2$
- **C.** $15$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $l=15\text{ cm},\ \frac{2970}{7}\ \text{cm}^2$

1. Slant height by Pythagoras. $ l = \sqrt{9^2 + 12^2} = \sqrt{225} = 15 $
2. Curved surface area = πrl. $ \frac{22}{7}\times 9\times 15 = \frac{2970}{7}\ \text{cm}^2 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $\frac{2376}{7}\ \text{cm}^2$ — Used vertical height h instead of l.
- **C** $15$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

### G11C06 — Binomial Expressions

#### QZ-G11C06-A-CUBE_IDENT-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C06-A-CUBE_IDENT-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C06-A-CUBE_IDENT-01", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C06", "value": 28, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-CUBE", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** If x + y = 4 and xy = 3, find the value of x³ + y³ without finding x and y.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$28$ ()

1. Expand (x + y)³. $ (x+y)^3 = x^3 + y^3 + 3xy(x+y) $
2. Rearrange. $ x^3 + y^3 = (x+y)^3 - 3xy(x+y) $
3. Substitute. $ = 4^3 - 3\times3\times4 = 64 - 36 = 28 $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C06-A-CUBE_IDENT-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C06-A-CUBE_IDENT-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C06-A-CUBE_IDENT-02", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C06", "value": 72, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-CUBE", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** If x + y = 6 and xy = 8, find the value of x³ + y³ without finding x and y.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$72$ ()

1. Expand (x + y)³. $ (x+y)^3 = x^3 + y^3 + 3xy(x+y) $
2. Rearrange. $ x^3 + y^3 = (x+y)^3 - 3xy(x+y) $
3. Substitute. $ = 6^3 - 3\times8\times6 = 216 - 144 = 72 $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C06-A-CUBE_IDENT-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C06-A-CUBE_IDENT-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C06-A-CUBE_IDENT-03", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C06", "value": 35, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-ALG-CUBE", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** If x + y = 5 and xy = 6, find the value of x³ + y³ without finding x and y.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$35$ ()

1. Expand (x + y)³. $ (x+y)^3 = x^3 + y^3 + 3xy(x+y) $
2. Rearrange. $ x^3 + y^3 = (x+y)^3 - 3xy(x+y) $
3. Substitute. $ = 5^3 - 3\times6\times5 = 125 - 90 = 35 $

</details>


### G11C07 — Algebraic Fractions

#### QZ-G11C07-G-AF_MULTIPL-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C07-G-AF_MULTIPL-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C07-G-AF_MULTIPL-01", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C07", "value": 0.5, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{x^2 + 2x}{x^2 - 4} \times \frac{x - 2}{2x}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$\frac{1}{2}$ ()

1. Factorise everything. $ \frac{x(x+2)}{(x+2)(x-2)}\times\frac{x-2}{2x} $
2. Cancel common factors. $ = \frac{1}{2} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C07-G-AF_MULTIPL-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C07-G-AF_MULTIPL-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C07-G-AF_MULTIPL-02", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C07", "value": 0.5, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{x^2 + 3x}{x^2 - 9} \times \frac{x - 3}{2x}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$\frac{1}{2}$ ()

1. Factorise everything. $ \frac{x(x+3)}{(x+3)(x-3)}\times\frac{x-3}{2x} $
2. Cancel common factors. $ = \frac{1}{2} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C07-G-AF_MULTIPL-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C07-G-AF_MULTIPL-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C07-G-AF_MULTIPL-03", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C07", "value": 0.5, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-AFRAC", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Simplify: $\frac{x^2 + 1x}{x^2 - 1} \times \frac{x - 1}{2x}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$\frac{1}{2}$ ()

1. Factorise everything. $ \frac{x(x+1)}{(x+1)(x-1)}\times\frac{x-1}{2x} $
2. Cancel common factors. $ = \frac{1}{2} $

</details>


### G11C08 — Areas of Plane Figures between Parallel Lines

#### QZ-G11C08-F-SAME_BASE_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C08-F-SAME_BASE_-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C08-F-SAME_BASE_-01", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C08", "value": 24, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "cm²", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-AREA-PAR", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** ΔABC and ΔDBC are on the same base BC and between the same parallel lines (AD ∥ BC). Area of ΔABC = 24 cm². Find the area of ΔDBC.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$24\ \text{cm}^2$ (Unit: cm²)

1. Same base, same height (distance between parallels). $ h_{ABC} = h_{DBC} $
2. So the areas are equal. $ \text{Area }\triangle DBC = 24\ \text{cm}^2 $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C08-G-PARA_TRIAN-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C08-G-PARA_TRIAN-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C08-G-PARA_TRIAN-01", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C08", "value": 45, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "cm²", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-AREA-PAR", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Parallelogram PQRS and ΔPQT are on the same base PQ and between the same parallels. The area of PQRS is 90 cm². Find the area of ΔPQT.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$45\ \text{cm}^2$ (Unit: cm²)

1. A triangle is half the parallelogram on the same base and between the same parallels. $ \triangle = \tfrac12\ \text{parallelogram} $
2. Calculate. $ \tfrac12\times 90 = 45\ \text{cm}^2 $

</details>

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$28\ \text{cm}^2$ (Unit: cm²)

1. A triangle is half the parallelogram on the same base and between the same parallels. $ \triangle = \tfrac12\ \text{parallelogram} $
2. Calculate. $ \tfrac12\times 56 = 28\ \text{cm}^2 $

</details>


### G11C09 — Percentage

#### QZ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C09", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-FIN-RATES", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** The annual value of a house is Rs. 24 000. The council charges rates of 8%. Find the rates per quarter.

- **A.** $\text{Rs. }1920$
- **B.** $1920$
- **C.** $\text{Rs. }480$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $\text{Rs. }480$

1. Annual rates. $ 24\,000\times\frac{8}{100} = 1920 $
2. One quarter = ÷ 4. $ 1920\div 4 = 480 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\text{Rs. }1920$ — Annual rates, not quarterly.
- **B** $1920$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C09", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-FIN-RATES", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->

- **A.** $2500$
- **B.** $\text{Rs. }2500$
- **C.** $\text{Rs. }625$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $\text{Rs. }625$

1. Annual rates. $ 50\,000\times\frac{5}{100} = 2500 $
2. One quarter = ÷ 4. $ 2500\div 4 = 625 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $2500$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $\text{Rs. }2500$ — Annual rates, not quarterly.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-04
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-04", "source_question": "MQ-G11C09-F-RATES_TAX-04", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C09", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-FIN-RATES", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** The annual value of a house is Rs. 36 000. The council charges rates of 6%. Find the rates per quarter.

- **A.** $2160$
- **B.** $\text{Rs. }540$
- **C.** $\text{Rs. }2160$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $\text{Rs. }540$

1. Annual rates. $ 36\,000\times\frac{6}{100} = 2160 $
2. One quarter = ÷ 4. $ 2160\div 4 = 540 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $2160$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $\text{Rs. }2160$ — Annual rates, not quarterly.

#### QZ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C09", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-FIN-CI", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Rs. 100 000 is deposited at 8% compound interest per year. Find the total amount after 2 years and the interest earned.

- **A.** $8000$
- **B.** $108\,000$
- **C.** $\text{Rs. }116\,000$
- **D.** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }116\,640,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }16\,640$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**D** — $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }116\,640,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }16\,640$

1. Interest for year 1. $ 100\,000\times\frac{8}{100} = 8000 $
2. Amount after year 1. $ 100\,000 + 8000 = 108\,000 $
3. Interest for year 2 is on the new amount. $ 108\,000\times\frac{8}{100} = 8640 $
4. Amount after year 2. $ 108\,000 + 8640 = 116\,640 $
5. Total interest. $ 116\,640 - 100\,000 = 16\,640 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $8000$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $108\,000$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $\text{Rs. }116\,000$ — Used simple interest.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C09", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-FIN-CI", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Rs. 40 000 is deposited at 5% compound interest per year. Find the total amount after 2 years and the interest earned.

- **A.** $2000$
- **B.** $42\,000$
- **C.** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }44\,100,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }4100$
- **D.** $\text{Rs. }44\,000$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }44\,100,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }4100$

1. Interest for year 1. $ 40\,000\times\frac{5}{100} = 2000 $
2. Amount after year 1. $ 40\,000 + 2000 = 42\,000 $
3. Interest for year 2 is on the new amount. $ 42\,000\times\frac{5}{100} = 2100 $
4. Amount after year 2. $ 42\,000 + 2100 = 44\,100 $
5. Total interest. $ 44\,100 - 40\,000 = 4100 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $2000$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $42\,000$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $\text{Rs. }44\,000$ — Used simple interest.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C09-G-COMPOUND_I-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C09", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-FIN-CI", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Rs. 50 000 is deposited at 10% compound interest per year. Find the total amount after 2 years and the interest earned.

- **A.** $\text{Rs. }60\,000$
- **B.** $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }60\,500,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }10\,500$
- **C.** $55\,000$
- **D.** $5000$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $\text{Amount }\text{Rs. }60\,500,\ \text{interest }\text{Rs. }10\,500$

1. Interest for year 1. $ 50\,000\times\frac{10}{100} = 5000 $
2. Amount after year 1. $ 50\,000 + 5000 = 55\,000 $
3. Interest for year 2 is on the new amount. $ 55\,000\times\frac{10}{100} = 5500 $
4. Amount after year 2. $ 55\,000 + 5500 = 60\,500 $
5. Total interest. $ 60\,500 - 50\,000 = 10\,500 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\text{Rs. }60\,000$ — Used simple interest.
- **C** $55\,000$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $5000$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

### G11C11 — Mid-point Theorem

#### QZ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C11", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "diagram": "GEO-MIDPT-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** In ΔABC, P and Q are the midpoints of AB and AC. BC = 8 cm. Find PQ.

- **A.** $\tfrac12 BC$
- **B.** $4\ \text{cm}$
- **C.** $16\ \text{cm}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $4\ \text{cm}$

1. Mid-point theorem: PQ ∥ BC and PQ = ½ BC. $ PQ = \tfrac12 BC $
2. Calculate. $ PQ = \tfrac12\times 8 = 4\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\tfrac12 BC$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $16\ \text{cm}$ — Doubled instead of halving.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C11", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "diagram": "GEO-MIDPT-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** In ΔABC, P and Q are the midpoints of AB and AC. BC = 12.4 cm. Find PQ.

- **A.** $\tfrac12 BC$
- **B.** $24.8\ \text{cm}$
- **C.** $6.2\ \text{cm}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $6.2\ \text{cm}$

1. Mid-point theorem: PQ ∥ BC and PQ = ½ BC. $ PQ = \tfrac12 BC $
2. Calculate. $ PQ = \tfrac12\times 12.4 = 6.2\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\tfrac12 BC$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $24.8\ \text{cm}$ — Doubled instead of halving.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-04
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-04", "source_question": "MQ-G11C11-F-MIDPOINT_L-04", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C11", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "diagram": "GEO-MIDPT-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** In ΔABC, P and Q are the midpoints of AB and AC. BC = 9 cm. Find PQ.

- **A.** $4.5\ \text{cm}$
- **B.** $18\ \text{cm}$
- **C.** $\tfrac12 BC$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $4.5\ \text{cm}$

1. Mid-point theorem: PQ ∥ BC and PQ = ½ BC. $ PQ = \tfrac12 BC $
2. Calculate. $ PQ = \tfrac12\times 9 = 4.5\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $18\ \text{cm}$ — Doubled instead of halving.
- **C** $\tfrac12 BC$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-01", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C11", "value": 14.0, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "cm", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** The sides of ΔABC are AB = 7 cm, BC = 9 cm and CA = 12 cm. P, Q, R are the midpoints of the sides. Find the perimeter of ΔPQR.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$14\ \text{cm}$ (Unit: cm)

1. Each side of ΔPQR is half of a side of ΔABC. $ \tfrac12(7),\ \tfrac12(9),\ \tfrac12(12) $
2. Perimeter = half of the big perimeter. $ \tfrac12(7+9+12) = 14\ \text{cm} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-02", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C11", "value": 12.0, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "cm", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "medium": "en"} -->

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$12\ \text{cm}$ (Unit: cm)

1. Each side of ΔPQR is half of a side of ΔABC. $ \tfrac12(6),\ \tfrac12(8),\ \tfrac12(10) $
2. Perimeter = half of the big perimeter. $ \tfrac12(6+8+10) = 12\ \text{cm} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C11-G-MIDPOINT_P-03", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C11", "value": 15.0, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "cm", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-MID", "medium": "en"} -->

$15\ \text{cm}$ (Unit: cm)

### G11C12 — Graphs

#### QZ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C12", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** For $y = (x  - 2)^2  - 5$, write down the turning point, the axis of symmetry and the minimum value of y.

- **A.** $(2,-5),\ x=2,\ y_{\min}=-5$
- **B.** $(2,\ -5)$
- **C.** $2$
- **D.** $(-2,-5),\ x=-2,\ y_{\min}=-5$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $(2,-5),\ x=2,\ y_{\min}=-5$

1. Compare with y = (x + b)² + c: turning point (−b, c). $ (-(-2),\ -5) = (2,\ -5) $
2. Axis of symmetry passes through the turning point. $ x = 2 $
3. (x + b)² ≥ 0, so the minimum is c. $ y_{\min} = -5 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $(2,\ -5)$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $(-2,-5),\ x=-2,\ y_{\min}=-5$ — Sign of the x-coordinate not flipped.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C12", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** For $y = (x  + 2)^2  - 3$, write down the turning point, the axis of symmetry and the minimum value of y.

- **A.** $-2$
- **B.** $(-2,-3),\ x=-2,\ y_{\min}=-3$
- **C.** $(-2,\ -3)$
- **D.** $(2,-3),\ x=2,\ y_{\min}=-3$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $(-2,-3),\ x=-2,\ y_{\min}=-3$

1. Compare with y = (x + b)² + c: turning point (−b, c). $ (-(2),\ -3) = (-2,\ -3) $
2. Axis of symmetry passes through the turning point. $ x = -2 $
3. (x + b)² ≥ 0, so the minimum is c. $ y_{\min} = -3 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $-2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $(-2,\ -3)$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $(2,-3),\ x=2,\ y_{\min}=-3$ — Sign of the x-coordinate not flipped.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04", "source_question": "MQ-G11C12-F-TP_FROM_VE-04", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C12", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GRA-VERTEX", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** For $y = (x  + 3)^2  - 1$, write down the turning point, the axis of symmetry and the minimum value of y.

- **A.** $(-3,-1),\ x=-3,\ y_{\min}=-1$
- **B.** $(3,-1),\ x=3,\ y_{\min}=-1$
- **C.** $(-3,\ -1)$
- **D.** $-3$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $(-3,-1),\ x=-3,\ y_{\min}=-1$

1. Compare with y = (x + b)² + c: turning point (−b, c). $ (-(3),\ -1) = (-3,\ -1) $
2. Axis of symmetry passes through the turning point. $ x = -3 $
3. (x + b)² ≥ 0, so the minimum is c. $ y_{\min} = -1 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $(3,-1),\ x=3,\ y_{\min}=-1$ — Sign of the x-coordinate not flipped.
- **C** $(-3,\ -1)$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $-3$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C12", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GRA-QUAD", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** For $y = x^{2} - 2x - 3$, prepare a table of values for x from -2 to 4, then use it to find (i) the roots of $x^{2} - 2x - 3 = 0$ (ii) the turning point.

- **A.** $x=-1,\ 3;\ (1,-4)$
- **B.** $1$
- **C.** $3$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $x=-1,\ 3;\ (1,-4)$

1. Table of values. $ \begin{array}{c|ccccccc} x & -2 & -1 & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4\\ \hline y & 5 & 0 & -3 & -4 & -3 & 0 & 5\end{array} $
2. Roots: where y = 0 (the curve cuts the x-axis). $ x = -1,\ x = 3 $
3. Turning point x-value = midway between the roots. $ x = \frac{-1+3}{2} = 1 $
4. Substitute to get y. $ y = -4 \Rightarrow (1,\ -4) $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $1$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $3$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C12", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GRA-QUAD", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** For $y = x^{2} - 4$, prepare a table of values for x from -3 to 3, then use it to find (i) the roots of $x^{2} - 4 = 0$ (ii) the turning point.

- **A.** $2$
- **B.** $x=-2,\ 2;\ (0,-4)$
- **C.** $0$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $x=-2,\ 2;\ (0,-4)$

1. Table of values. $ \begin{array}{c|ccccccc} x & -3 & -2 & -1 & 0 & 1 & 2 & 3\\ \hline y & 5 & 0 & -3 & -4 & -3 & 0 & 5\end{array} $
2. Roots: where y = 0 (the curve cuts the x-axis). $ x = -2,\ x = 2 $
3. Turning point x-value = midway between the roots. $ x = \frac{-2+2}{2} = 0 $
4. Substitute to get y. $ y = -4 \Rightarrow (0,\ -4) $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $0$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C12-G-QUAD_TABLE-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C12", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GRA-QUAD", "diagram": "GRA-QUAD-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** For $y = x^{2} + 2x - 3$, prepare a table of values for x from -4 to 2, then use it to find (i) the roots of $x^{2} + 2x - 3 = 0$ (ii) the turning point.

- **A.** $1$
- **B.** $-1$
- **C.** $x=-3,\ 1;\ (-1,-4)$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $x=-3,\ 1;\ (-1,-4)$

1. Table of values. $ \begin{array}{c|ccccccc} x & -4 & -3 & -2 & -1 & 0 & 1 & 2\\ \hline y & 5 & 0 & -3 & -4 & -3 & 0 & 5\end{array} $
2. Roots: where y = 0 (the curve cuts the x-axis). $ x = -3,\ x = 1 $
3. Turning point x-value = midway between the roots. $ x = \frac{-3+1}{2} = -1 $
4. Substitute to get y. $ y = -4 \Rightarrow (-1,\ -4) $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $1$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $-1$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>


### G11C13 — Equations

#### QZ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C13", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Solve: $x^{2} - 7x + 10 = 0$

- **A.** $x = -2\ \text{or}\ -5$
- **B.** $0$
- **C.** $x = 2\ \text{or}\ 5$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $x = 2\ \text{or}\ 5$

1. Factorise. $ (x  - 2)(x  - 5) = 0 $
2. Each factor = 0. $ x  - 2 = 0\ \text{or}\ x  - 5 = 0 $
3. Solve. $ x = 2\ \text{or}\ x = 5 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $x = -2\ \text{or}\ -5$ — Signs of the roots reversed.
- **B** $0$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C13", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Solve: $x^{2} + 5x - 6 = 0$

- **A.** $0$
- **B.** $x = 1\ \text{or}\ -6$
- **C.** $x = -1\ \text{or}\ 6$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $x = 1\ \text{or}\ -6$

1. Factorise. $ (x  - 1)(x  + 6) = 0 $
2. Each factor = 0. $ x  - 1 = 0\ \text{or}\ x  + 6 = 0 $
3. Solve. $ x = 1\ \text{or}\ x = -6 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $0$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $x = -1\ \text{or}\ 6$ — Signs of the roots reversed.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C13", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Solve: $x^{2} + 7x + 10 = 0$

- **A.** $x = -2\ \text{or}\ -5$
- **B.** $0$
- **C.** $x = 2\ \text{or}\ 5$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $x = -2\ \text{or}\ -5$

1. Factorise. $ (x  + 2)(x  + 5) = 0 $
2. Each factor = 0. $ x  + 2 = 0\ \text{or}\ x  + 5 = 0 $
3. Solve. $ x = -2\ \text{or}\ x = -5 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $0$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $x = 2\ \text{or}\ 5$ — Signs of the roots reversed.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-04
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-04", "source_question": "MQ-G11C13-F-QUAD_FACTO-04", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C13", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-ALG-QUAD", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Solve: $x^{2} - x - 12 = 0$

- **A.** $0$
- **B.** $x = -3\ \text{or}\ 4$
- **C.** $x = 3\ \text{or}\ -4$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $x = -3\ \text{or}\ 4$

1. Factorise. $ (x  + 3)(x  - 4) = 0 $
2. Each factor = 0. $ x  + 3 = 0\ \text{or}\ x  - 4 = 0 $
3. Solve. $ x = -3\ \text{or}\ x = 4 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $0$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $x = 3\ \text{or}\ -4$ — Signs of the roots reversed.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C13", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-QFORM", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Solve $x^{2} + 2x - 4 = 0$ using the formula. Give answers to 2 decimal places.

- **A.** $20$
- **B.** $x \approx 1.24,\ -3.24$
- **C.** $-4$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $x \approx 1.24,\ -3.24$

1. Identify a, b, c. $ a = 1,\ b = 2,\ c = -4 $
2. Discriminant b² − 4ac. $ (2)^2 - 4(1)(-4) = 20 $
3. Formula. $ x = \frac{-(2) \pm \sqrt{20}}{2\times1},\quad \sqrt{20} \approx 4.472 $
4. Two answers. $ x \approx 1.24\ \text{or}\ x \approx -3.24 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $20$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $-4$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C13-G-QUAD_FORMU-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C13", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-ALG-QFORM", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Solve $x^{2} - 4x + 1 = 0$ using the formula. Give answers to 2 decimal places.

- **A.** $12$
- **B.** $1$
- **C.** $x \approx 3.73,\ 0.27$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $x \approx 3.73,\ 0.27$

1. Identify a, b, c. $ a = 1,\ b = -4,\ c = 1 $
2. Discriminant b² − 4ac. $ (-4)^2 - 4(1)(1) = 12 $
3. Formula. $ x = \frac{-(-4) \pm \sqrt{12}}{2\times1},\quad \sqrt{12} \approx 3.464 $
4. Two answers. $ x \approx 3.73\ \text{or}\ x \approx 0.27 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $12$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $1$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>


### G11C14 — Equiangular Triangles

#### QZ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-01", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C14", "value": 15.0, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "cm", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** ΔABC and ΔPQR are equiangular. AB = 3 cm, BC = 5 cm and PQ = 9 cm. Find QR.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$15\ \text{cm}$ (Unit: cm)

1. Corresponding sides are in the same ratio. $ \frac{PQ}{AB} = \frac{QR}{BC} $
2. Substitute. $ \frac{9}{3} = \frac{QR}{5} $
3. Solve. $ QR = 5\times3 = 15\ \text{cm} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-02", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C14", "value": 4.5, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "cm", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** ΔABC and ΔPQR are equiangular. AB = 6 cm, BC = 9 cm and PQ = 3 cm. Find QR.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$\frac{9}{2}\ \text{cm}$ (Unit: cm)

1. Corresponding sides are in the same ratio. $ \frac{PQ}{AB} = \frac{QR}{BC} $
2. Substitute. $ \frac{3}{6} = \frac{QR}{9} $
3. Solve. $ QR = 9\times\frac{1}{2} = \frac{9}{2}\ \text{cm} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-03", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C14", "value": 12.0, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "cm", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** ΔABC and ΔPQR are equiangular. AB = 4 cm, BC = 6 cm and PQ = 8 cm. Find QR.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$12\ \text{cm}$ (Unit: cm)

1. Corresponding sides are in the same ratio. $ \frac{PQ}{AB} = \frac{QR}{BC} $
2. Substitute. $ \frac{8}{4} = \frac{QR}{6} $
3. Solve. $ QR = 6\times2 = 12\ \text{cm} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04", "source_question": "MQ-G11C14-F-SIMILAR_RA-04", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C14", "value": 10.5, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "cm", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** ΔABC and ΔPQR are equiangular. AB = 5 cm, BC = 7 cm and PQ = \frac{15}{2} cm. Find QR.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$\frac{21}{2}\ \text{cm}$ (Unit: cm)

1. Corresponding sides are in the same ratio. $ \frac{PQ}{AB} = \frac{QR}{BC} $
2. Substitute. $ \frac{\frac{15}{2}}{5} = \frac{QR}{7} $
3. Solve. $ QR = 7\times\frac{3}{2} = \frac{21}{2}\ \text{cm} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C14", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "diagram": "GEO-SIMILAR-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** In ΔABC, DE ∥ BC with D on AB and E on AC. AD = 3 cm, DB = 6 cm and DE = 5 cm. Find BC.

- **A.** $15\ \text{cm}$
- **B.** $9$
- **C.** $10\ \text{cm}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $15\ \text{cm}$

1. ΔADE and ΔABC are equiangular (corresponding angles, DE ∥ BC). $ \triangle ADE \sim \triangle ABC $
2. AB = AD + DB. $ AB = 3+6 = 9 $
3. Ratio of sides. $ \frac{BC}{DE} = \frac{AB}{AD} \Rightarrow BC = 5\times\frac{9}{3} = 15\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $9$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $10\ \text{cm}$ — Used DB instead of AB.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C14-G-SIMILAR_PA-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C14", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-SIM", "diagram": "GEO-SIMILAR-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** In ΔABC, DE ∥ BC with D on AB and E on AC. AD = 5 cm, DB = 3 cm and DE = 10 cm. Find BC.

- **A.** $16\ \text{cm}$
- **B.** $8$
- **C.** $6\ \text{cm}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $16\ \text{cm}$

1. ΔADE and ΔABC are equiangular (corresponding angles, DE ∥ BC). $ \triangle ADE \sim \triangle ABC $
2. AB = AD + DB. $ AB = 5+3 = 8 $
3. Ratio of sides. $ \frac{BC}{DE} = \frac{AB}{AD} \Rightarrow BC = 10\times\frac{8}{5} = 16\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $8$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $6\ \text{cm}$ — Used DB instead of AB.

</details>


### G11C15 — Data Representation and Prediction

#### QZ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C15-F-MEDIAN_POS-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C15", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-STA-MEDIAN", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Find the median of: 13, 21, 35, 37, 44, 49, 58

- **A.** $7$
- **B.** $4$
- **C.** $37$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $37$

1. The data is already in order. Count the values. $ n = 7 $
2. Median position = (n + 1)/2. $ \frac{7+1}{2} = 4 $
3. Read that value. $ \text{median} = 37 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $7$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $4$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C15", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-STA-QUART", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** For the 11 values 6, 28, 29, 30, 32, 38, 43, 48, 51, 59, 74, find the lower quartile, the upper quartile and the interquartile range.

- **A.** $Q_1=29,\ Q_3=51,\ \text{IQR}=22$
- **B.** $29$
- **C.** $51$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $Q_1=29,\ Q_3=51,\ \text{IQR}=22$

1. Q₁ position = (n + 1)/4. $ \frac{11+1}{4} = 3 \Rightarrow Q_1 = 29 $
2. Q₃ position = 3(n + 1)/4. $ \frac{3(11+1)}{4} = 9 \Rightarrow Q_3 = 51 $
3. Interquartile range = Q₃ − Q₁. $ 51 - 29 = 22 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $29$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $51$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C15-G-QUARTILES-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C15", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-STA-QUART", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** For the 11 values 13, 17, 22, 28, 36, 47, 48, 55, 63, 73, 74, find the lower quartile, the upper quartile and the interquartile range.

- **A.** $22$
- **B.** $Q_1=22,\ Q_3=63,\ \text{IQR}=41$
- **C.** $63$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $Q_1=22,\ Q_3=63,\ \text{IQR}=41$

1. Q₁ position = (n + 1)/4. $ \frac{11+1}{4} = 3 \Rightarrow Q_1 = 22 $
2. Q₃ position = 3(n + 1)/4. $ \frac{3(11+1)}{4} = 9 \Rightarrow Q_3 = 63 $
3. Interquartile range = Q₃ − Q₁. $ 63 - 22 = 41 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $22$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $63$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

### G11C16 — Geometric Progressions

#### QZ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C16-F-GP_NTH-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C16", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-GP-TN", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Find the 5th term of the geometric progression 2, 6, 18, …

- **A.** $3$
- **B.** $162$
- **C.** $486$
- **D.** $2\times(3)^{4}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $162$

1. Common ratio r = second ÷ first. $ r = 3 $
2. Tₙ = arⁿ⁻¹. $ T_{5} = 2\times(3)^{4} $
3. Calculate. $ = 2\times 81 = 162 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $3$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $486$ — Used power n instead of n − 1.
- **D** $2\times(3)^{4}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C16-G-GP_SUM-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C16-G-GP_SUM-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C16", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-GP-SN", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->

### G11C17 — Pythagoras' Theorem

#### QZ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C17", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-PYTH", "diagram": "GEO-RIGHT-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** The two shorter sides of a right-angled triangle are 6 cm and 8 cm. Find the hypotenuse.

- **A.** $10\ \text{cm}$
- **B.** $14\ \text{cm}$
- **C.** $100$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $10\ \text{cm}$

1. c² = a² + b². $ c^2 = 6^2 + 8^2 = 36 + 64 = 100 $
2. Square root. $ c = \sqrt{100} = 10\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $14\ \text{cm}$ — Added the sides without squaring.
- **C** $100$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C17-F-PYTH_HYP-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C17", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-GEO-PYTH", "diagram": "GEO-RIGHT-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** The two shorter sides of a right-angled triangle are 8 cm and 15 cm. Find the hypotenuse.

- **A.** $17\ \text{cm}$
- **B.** $23\ \text{cm}$
- **C.** $289$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $17\ \text{cm}$

1. c² = a² + b². $ c^2 = 8^2 + 15^2 = 64 + 225 = 289 $
2. Square root. $ c = \sqrt{289} = 17\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $23\ \text{cm}$ — Added the sides without squaring.
- **C** $289$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

- **A** $169$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C17-G-PYTH_LADDE-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C17", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-GEO-PYTH", "diagram": "GEO-RIGHT-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** A 17 m ladder leans against a vertical wall. Its foot is 8 m from the wall. How high up the wall does it reach?

- **A.** $17^2$
- **B.** $15\ \text{m}$
- **C.** $225$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $15\ \text{m}$

1. The ladder is the hypotenuse. $ h^2 + 8^2 = 17^2 $
2. Rearrange. $ h^2 = 289 - 64 = 225 $
3. Square root. $ h = 15\ \text{m} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $17^2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $225$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

- **B.** $13^2$
- **C.** $144$

**A** — $12\ \text{m}$

1. The ladder is the hypotenuse. $ h^2 + 5^2 = 13^2 $
2. Rearrange. $ h^2 = 169 - 25 = 144 $
3. Square root. $ h = 12\ \text{m} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $13^2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $144$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>


### G11C18 — Trigonometry

#### QZ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C18-F-TRIG_RATIO-02", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C18", "value": 0.47058823529411764, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-TRI-RATIOS", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** In right-angled ΔABC, ∠B = 90°, AB = 15 cm, BC = 8 cm and AC = 17 cm. Write sin A, cos A and tan A.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

1. For angle A: opposite = BC, adjacent = AB, hypotenuse = AC. $ \text{opp}=BC,\ \text{adj}=AB,\ \text{hyp}=AC $
2. Ratios. $ \sin A = \frac{8}{17},\ \cos A = \frac{15}{17},\ \tan A = \frac{8}{15} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C18-G-ELEVATION_-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C18", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-TRI-RATIOS", "diagram": "TRI-ELEV-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** From a point 20 m from the foot of a vertical tower on level ground, the angle of elevation of the top is 35°. Find the height of the tower. (Use tables: tan 35° = 0.7002)

- **A.** $\frac{h}{20}$
- **B.** $20\times 0.7002$
- **C.** $14.00\ \text{m}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $14.00\ \text{m}$

1. Draw a right triangle: height opposite the angle, distance adjacent. $ \tan 35^\circ = \frac{h}{20} $
2. Make h the subject. $ h = 20\times\tan 35^\circ = 20\times 0.7002 $
3. Calculate. $ h \approx 14.00\ \text{m} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\frac{h}{20}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $20\times 0.7002$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

### G11C19 — Matrices

#### QZ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C19", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MAT-MULT", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Find x and y if $\begin{pmatrix} 3 & 2 \\ 1 & -1 \end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix} x \\ y \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 5 \\ -5 \end{pmatrix}$.

- **A.** $4$
- **B.** $x=-1,\ y=4$
- **C.** $-5$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $x=-1,\ y=4$

1. Multiply out. $ 3x  + 2y = 5,\quad 1x  - 1y = -5 $
2. Solve the simultaneous equations. $ x = -1,\ y = 4 $
3. Check. $ 3(-1)  + 2(4) = 5\ \checkmark $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $4$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $-5$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C19", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MAT-MULT", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Find x and y if $\begin{pmatrix} 2 & 1 \\ 1 & 3 \end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix} x \\ y \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 4 \\ -3 \end{pmatrix}$.

- **A.** $-3$
- **B.** $x=3,\ y=-2$
- **C.** $-2$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $x=3,\ y=-2$

1. Multiply out. $ 2x  + 1y = 4,\quad 1x  + 3y = -3 $
2. Solve the simultaneous equations. $ x = 3,\ y = -2 $
3. Check. $ 2(3)  + 1(-2) = 4\ \checkmark $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $-3$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $-2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C19-A-MAT_EQUATI-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C19", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-MAT-MULT", "diagram": null, "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Find x and y if $\begin{pmatrix} 1 & 2 \\ 2 & 5 \end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix} x \\ y \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} 7 \\ 18 \end{pmatrix}$.

- **A.** $4$
- **B.** $18$
- **C.** $x=-1,\ y=4$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $x=-1,\ y=4$

1. Multiply out. $ 1x  + 2y = 7,\quad 2x  + 5y = 18 $
2. Solve the simultaneous equations. $ x = -1,\ y = 4 $
3. Check. $ 1(-1)  + 2(4) = 7\ \checkmark $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $4$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $18$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

### G11C21 — Cyclic Quadrilaterals

#### QZ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C21-F-CYCLIC_OPP-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C21", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-CIR-CYCLIC", "diagram": "GEO-CYCLIC-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** ABCD is a cyclic quadrilateral. ∠A = 70° and ∠B = 95°. Find ∠C and ∠D.

- **A.** $\angle C=110^\circ,\ \angle D=85^\circ$
- **B.** $110^\circ$
- **C.** $\angle C=70^\circ,\ \angle D=95^\circ$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $\angle C=110^\circ,\ \angle D=85^\circ$

1. Opposite angles of a cyclic quadrilateral add to 180°. $ \angle C = 180 - 70 = 110^\circ $
2. Same for B and D. $ \angle D = 180 - 95 = 85^\circ $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $110^\circ$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $\angle C=70^\circ,\ \angle D=95^\circ$ — Assumed opposite angles are equal (that is for parallelograms).

</details>

</details>

### G11C22 — Tangents

#### QZ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C22", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "diagram": "GEO-TANGENT-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** A tangent is drawn from a point P to a circle with centre O and radius 5 cm. OP = 13 cm. Find the length of the tangent.

- **A.** $12\ \text{cm}$
- **B.** $144$
- **C.** $90^\circ$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $12\ \text{cm}$

1. Tangent ⟂ radius at the point of contact. $ \angle OTP = 90^\circ $
2. Pythagoras. $ PT^2 = 13^2 - 5^2 = 144 $
3. Square root. $ PT = 12\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $144$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $90^\circ$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C22", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "diagram": "GEO-TANGENT-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** A tangent is drawn from a point P to a circle with centre O and radius 7 cm. OP = 25 cm. Find the length of the tangent.

- **A.** $24\ \text{cm}$
- **B.** $90^\circ$
- **C.** $576$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $24\ \text{cm}$

1. Tangent ⟂ radius at the point of contact. $ \angle OTP = 90^\circ $
2. Pythagoras. $ PT^2 = 25^2 - 7^2 = 576 $
3. Square root. $ PT = 24\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $90^\circ$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $576$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C22", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "diagram": "GEO-TANGENT-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** A tangent is drawn from a point P to a circle with centre O and radius 8 cm. OP = 17 cm. Find the length of the tangent.

- **A.** $90^\circ$
- **B.** $225$
- **C.** $15\ \text{cm}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $15\ \text{cm}$

1. Tangent ⟂ radius at the point of contact. $ \angle OTP = 90^\circ $
2. Pythagoras. $ PT^2 = 17^2 - 8^2 = 225 $
3. Square root. $ PT = 15\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $90^\circ$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $225$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-04
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-04", "source_question": "MQ-G11C22-F-TANGENT_RA-04", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C22", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "diagram": "GEO-TANGENT-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** A tangent is drawn from a point P to a circle with centre O and radius 6 cm. OP = 10 cm. Find the length of the tangent.

- **A.** $64$
- **B.** $8\ \text{cm}$
- **C.** $90^\circ$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $8\ \text{cm}$

1. Tangent ⟂ radius at the point of contact. $ \angle OTP = 90^\circ $
2. Pythagoras. $ PT^2 = 10^2 - 6^2 = 64 $
3. Square root. $ PT = 8\ \text{cm} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $64$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $90^\circ$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-01", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C22", "value": 130, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "°", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** PA and PB are tangents from P to a circle with centre O. ∠APB = 50°. Find ∠AOB and ∠PAB.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$\angle AOB=130^\circ,\ \angle PAB=65^\circ$ (Unit: °)

1. ∠OAP = ∠OBP = 90° (tangent ⟂ radius). $ 90^\circ + 90^\circ $
2. Angles of quadrilateral OAPB add to 360°. $ \angle AOB = 360 - 180 - 50 = 130^\circ $
3. PA = PB, so ΔPAB is isosceles. $ \angle PAB = \frac{180-50}{2} = 65^\circ $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C22-G-TWO_TANGEN-02", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C22", "value": 140, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "°", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "T-CIR-TAN", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** PA and PB are tangents from P to a circle with centre O. ∠APB = 40°. Find ∠AOB and ∠PAB.

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$\angle AOB=140^\circ,\ \angle PAB=70^\circ$ (Unit: °)

1. ∠OAP = ∠OBP = 90° (tangent ⟂ radius). $ 90^\circ + 90^\circ $
2. Angles of quadrilateral OAPB add to 360°. $ \angle AOB = 360 - 180 - 40 = 140^\circ $
3. PA = PB, so ΔPAB is isosceles. $ \angle PAB = \frac{180-40}{2} = 70^\circ $

</details>

### G11C24 — Sets

#### QZ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C24-F-SET_NOTATI-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C24", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-SET-UNION", "diagram": "SET-VENN2-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** U = {1, 2, …, 9}, A = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5}, B = {4, 5, 6, 7}. Find A ∩ B, A ∪ B and A′.

- **A.** $\{1,2,3,4,5,6,7\}$
- **B.** $A\cap B=\{4,5\};\ A\cup B=\{1,\dots,7\};\ A'=\{6,7,8,9\}$
- **C.** $\{4,5\}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**B** — $A\cap B=\{4,5\};\ A\cup B=\{1,\dots,7\};\ A'=\{6,7,8,9\}$

1. Intersection: elements in both. $ A\cap B = \{4,5\} $
2. Union: elements in either. $ A\cup B = \{1,2,3,4,5,6,7\} $
3. Complement: in U but not in A. $ A' = \{6,7,8,9\} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\{1,2,3,4,5,6,7\}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $\{4,5\}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C24", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-SET-UNION3", "diagram": "SET-VENN3-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** In a group of 50, 20 study Maths (M), 22 Science (S) and 18 English (E). 8 study M and S, 6 study S and E, 5 study M and E, and 3 study all three. Find (i) the number who study only Maths (ii) the number who study none.

- **A.** $3$
- **B.** $2$
- **C.** $10$
- **D.** $(i)\ 10\quad(ii)\ 6$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**D** — $(i)\ 10\quad(ii)\ 6$

1. Centre region (all three). $ n(M\cap S\cap E) = 3 $
2. Exactly two subjects (subtract the centre). $ MS: 8-3=5,\ SE: 6-3=3,\ ME: 5-3=2 $
3. Only Maths. $ 20 - 5 - 2 - 3 = 10 $
4. Only S and only E similarly. $ 11,\ 10 $
5. None = total − all regions. $ 50 - 44 = 6 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $3$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $10$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C24-G-THREE_SETS-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C24", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-SET-UNION3", "diagram": "SET-VENN3-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** In a group of 40, 15 study Maths (M), 18 Science (S) and 16 English (E). 5 study M and S, 4 study S and E, 6 study M and E, and 2 study all three. Find (i) the number who study only Maths (ii) the number who study none.

- **A.** $6$
- **B.** $4$
- **C.** $(i)\ 6\quad(ii)\ 4$
- **D.** $2$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**C** — $(i)\ 6\quad(ii)\ 4$

1. Centre region (all three). $ n(M\cap S\cap E) = 2 $
2. Exactly two subjects (subtract the centre). $ MS: 5-2=3,\ SE: 4-2=2,\ ME: 6-2=4 $
3. Only Maths. $ 15 - 3 - 4 - 2 = 6 $
4. Only S and only E similarly. $ 11,\ 8 $
5. None = total − all regions. $ 40 - 36 = 4 $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $6$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $4$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $2$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>


### G11C25 — Probability

#### QZ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-02", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C25", "value": 0.08333333333333333, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-PRO-INDEP", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Events A and B are independent with P(A) = \frac{1}{2} and P(B) = \frac{1}{6}. Find P(A and B).

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$\frac{1}{12}$ ()

1. Independent → multiply. $ P(A\cap B) = \frac{1}{2}\times\frac{1}{6} = \frac{1}{12} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C25-F-INDEP_AND-03", "type": "numeric", "chapter": "G11C25", "value": 0.2, "tolerance": 0, "unit": "", "pathway": "Foundation Mastery", "difficulty": "easy", "formula": "F-PRO-INDEP", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Events A and B are independent with P(A) = \frac{2}{5} and P(B) = \frac{1}{2}. Find P(A and B).

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

$\frac{1}{5}$ ()

1. Independent → multiply. $ P(A\cap B) = \frac{2}{5}\times\frac{1}{2} = \frac{1}{5} $

</details>

#### QZ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02", "source_question": "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-02", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C25", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-PRO-TREE", "diagram": "PRO-TREE-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Bag 1 has 3 red and 2 blue balls; Bag 2 has 3 red and 1 blue balls. One ball is taken from each bag. Using a tree diagram, find the probability that both balls are the same colour.

- **A.** $\frac{11}{20}$
- **B.** $\frac{9}{20}$
- **C.** $\frac{2}{5}$
- **D.** $\frac{1}{4}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $\frac{11}{20}$

1. First branches (Bag 1). $ P(R_1)=\frac{3}{5},\ P(B_1)=\frac{2}{5} $
2. Second branches (Bag 2). $ P(R_2)=\frac{3}{4},\ P(B_2)=\frac{1}{4} $
3. Both red: multiply along the branch. $ \frac{3}{5}\times\frac{3}{4} = \frac{9}{20} $
4. Both blue. $ \frac{2}{5}\times\frac{1}{4} = \frac{1}{10} $
5. Same colour: add the two outcomes. $ \frac{9}{20} + \frac{1}{10} = \frac{11}{20} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $\frac{9}{20}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $\frac{2}{5}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $\frac{1}{4}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-03
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-03", "source_question": "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-03", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C25", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-PRO-TREE", "diagram": "PRO-TREE-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Bag 1 has 3 red and 2 blue balls; Bag 2 has 2 red and 2 blue balls. One ball is taken from each bag. Using a tree diagram, find the probability that both balls are the same colour.

- **A.** $\frac{1}{2}$
- **B.** $\frac{1}{5}$
- **C.** $\frac{3}{10}$
- **D.** $\frac{2}{5}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $\frac{1}{2}$

1. First branches (Bag 1). $ P(R_1)=\frac{3}{5},\ P(B_1)=\frac{2}{5} $
2. Second branches (Bag 2). $ P(R_2)=\frac{1}{2},\ P(B_2)=\frac{1}{2} $
3. Both red: multiply along the branch. $ \frac{3}{5}\times\frac{1}{2} = \frac{3}{10} $
4. Both blue. $ \frac{2}{5}\times\frac{1}{2} = \frac{1}{5} $
5. Same colour: add the two outcomes. $ \frac{3}{10} + \frac{1}{5} = \frac{1}{2} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $\frac{1}{5}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $\frac{3}{10}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **D** $\frac{2}{5}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-04
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-04", "source_question": "MQ-G11C25-G-TREE_TWO_B-04", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C25", "pathway": "Grade Improvement", "difficulty": "medium", "formula": "F-PRO-TREE", "diagram": "PRO-TREE-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** Bag 1 has 2 red and 3 blue balls; Bag 2 has 3 red and 1 blue balls. One ball is taken from each bag. Using a tree diagram, find the probability that both balls are the same colour.

- **A.** $\frac{1}{4}$
- **B.** $\frac{3}{10}$
- **C.** $\frac{3}{5}$
- **D.** $\frac{9}{20}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**D** — $\frac{9}{20}$

1. First branches (Bag 1). $ P(R_1)=\frac{2}{5},\ P(B_1)=\frac{3}{5} $
2. Second branches (Bag 2). $ P(R_2)=\frac{3}{4},\ P(B_2)=\frac{1}{4} $
3. Both red: multiply along the branch. $ \frac{2}{5}\times\frac{3}{4} = \frac{3}{10} $
4. Both blue. $ \frac{3}{5}\times\frac{1}{4} = \frac{3}{20} $
5. Same colour: add the two outcomes. $ \frac{3}{10} + \frac{3}{20} = \frac{9}{20} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **A** $\frac{1}{4}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **B** $\frac{3}{10}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.
- **C** $\frac{3}{5}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>

#### QZ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01
<!-- meta {"id": "QZ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01", "source_question": "MQ-G11C25-A-TREE_WITHO-01", "type": "mcq", "chapter": "G11C25", "pathway": "A Grade Mastery", "difficulty": "hard", "formula": "F-PRO-TREE", "diagram": "PRO-TREE-01", "medium": "en"} -->
**Question:** A box has 4 red and 4 blue pens. Two pens are taken one after the other WITHOUT replacement. Find the probability that the two pens are of different colours.

- **A.** $\frac{4}{7}$
- **B.** $\frac{1}{2}$
- **C.** $\frac{2}{7}$

<details><summary>Correct answer</summary>

**A** — $\frac{4}{7}$

1. Second draw has one fewer pen. $ \text{total}: 8 \to 7 $
2. Red then blue. $ \frac{4}{8}\times\frac{4}{7} = \frac{2}{7} $
3. Blue then red. $ \frac{4}{8}\times\frac{4}{7} = \frac{2}{7} $
4. Add. $ \frac{2}{7} + \frac{2}{7} = \frac{4}{7} $

**Why the other options are wrong:**
- **B** $\frac{1}{2}$ — Treated it as WITH replacement.
- **C** $\frac{2}{7}$ — This is an intermediate value from the working — the question asks for the final result.

</details>
